摘要：

##### **A Generic On-Board AI Processing Chain for Earth Observation: From Application Development to Embedded Deployment and Mission-Level Orchestration**
2610.26340v1 by Michel-Fran{\c c}ois Foulon, Liz Ramos Medina, Thomas Goudemant, Clotilde Szywala, Virgile Robles, Cl{é}ment Petitjean, Ad{è}le Hankache, Omar Hlimi

Earth observation satellites acquire more data than they can downlink. Extracting information on board with artificial intelligence (AI) turns the satellite into a data-reduction stage that downlinks compact products, such as masks or detections, instead of full scenes; deploying it in a mission, however, involves the whole processing chain and not only the neural network. We present a generic on-board AI processing chain along three axes, with wildfire segmentation as the worked example among ship detection and cloud segmentation. (i) The AI model is tuned (spectral-band selection, input normalization), quantized to 8-bit integers (int8) and ported to an AMD Versal VCK190, with its excellent segmentation performance preserved on the board. (ii) A modular tiler-inference-mosaicer workflow, reused unchanged across applications, brings the tile-level model to scene level, turning a full acquisition into a scene-level mask or detection list. (iii) Each step is packaged as a unikernel and orchestrated on board by ORCHIDE, a cloud-native orchestrator. The chain is characterized end to end, from the standalone execution on the target to its execution under orchestration with parallel workflows on a representative test bench. Beyond AI inference on individual tiles, this approach aims at increasing the maturity of on-board AI deployment by addressing a complete processing chain, from the acquired scene to the mission-level execution.

摘要：

##### **Large-Scale Biomedical RAG: Corpus Scaling, Document Representation, and Efficiency Trade-offs**
2610.26339v1 by Adrián Sánchez-Freire, Gonzalo Pérez-Gómez, Aitziber Atutxa, Ander Barrena

We present a controlled, large-scale evaluation of how corpus scale, document representation, and reranking affect the effectiveness and efficiency of biomedical Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems. Retrieval collections were progressively expanded while retaining the relevant source documents, enabling consistent comparisons across corpus sizes. We compared abstract, flat full-text, and hierarchical full-text representations across first-stage retrieval, reranking, and end-to-end multiple-choice question answering. Configurations selected on development data were evaluated on five external biomedical benchmarks and a held-out test set, while retrieval latency and index storage were measured to quantify operational cost. Performance patterns observed with small or controlled collections did not consistently persist as collections grew, and the usefulness of reranking depended on the corpus and retrieval setting. RAG improved three of four model configurations across the external benchmarks, with mean gains of up to 9.6 percentage points, and improved all four configurations on the held-out test set by 10.2-30.3 points. Abstract retrieval performed best when reference evidence was abstract-aligned, whereas full-text representations were generally more effective when richer evidence was needed. Hierarchical representation matched or outperformed flat full-text retrieval in most end-to-end comparisons while requiring 67% fewer indexed units and 44-56% less storage. These findings show that corpus scale should be treated as an explicit evaluation variable in biomedical RAG, and that the most appropriate design depends on evidence format, model behavior, and operational constraints.

摘要：

##### **CIS-Transolver: A Cross-Layer Incremental-State Neural Operator for Fine-Scale Automotive Aerodynamic Flow Prediction**
2610.26337v1 by Jianglong Liu, Zhicheng Zhu, Jia Hao, Liangyue Jia

Neural operators offer an efficient data-driven approach to rapid prediction of complex automotive aerodynamic flows. However, spectral bias and other factors limit accurate reconstruction of fine-scale flow variations near complex geometries. Existing studies improve the representation and fitting of fine-scale information mainly through information extraction and training objectives. Further study is needed to determine how extracted local details can be used continually in subsequent operator computations and contribute effectively to the overall flow-field representation. We propose CIS-Transolver, a cross-layer incremental-state neural operator that extends fine-scale modeling from local information extraction to its use across layers. The method extracts fine-scale information through local multiscale analysis and encodes it as geometric moment states that are propagated and updated across layers. By combining information from previous layers with feedback to the value representations in Physics-Attention, the method allows local details to contribute continually to subsequent modeling of global physical correlations. We systematically evaluate CIS-Transolver on three public automotive aerodynamics benchmarks, ShapeNet-Car, AhmedML, and DrivAerNet++, covering surface pressure fields and volumetric pressure and velocity fields. CIS-Transolver achieves the lowest mean relative L2 error among the evaluated methods for all five prediction metrics across the three benchmarks. The relative error reductions average 8.72% across the five metrics compared with the best baseline for each metric, reaching 16.38% for ShapeNet-Car pressure prediction and 15.60% for AhmedML velocity prediction. These results provide a new approach to fine-scale flow prediction for complex geometries.

摘要：

##### **Typed Decision Models under Prevalence Shift: Decision Training, Calibration, and Implicit Priors**
2610.26325v1 by Abdul Rahman Iqbal, Muhammad Fawad Nasim, Amir Saleem

Typed decision models (TDMs) return a probability over caller-defined options, and Jev is described by its vendor as calibrated. But in deployment class frequencies change, so calibration measured on a fixed test set need not hold where thresholds are applied. Existing evaluations leave two gaps: the gains of vendor decision training have not been separated from those of the backbone under an identical readout, and TDMs have been tested only at the test set's class distribution. We ask whether decision training improves a model over its own backbone, and whether a TDM's calibration and thresholds withstand prevalence (label) shift as well as a fine-tuned classifier's. We compare three TDMs (Jev, Tev1-4B, Nimble-9B), their base models and two trained classifiers on identical decisions from five public datasets under simulated shift, with 35 paired tests fixed before any test was computed. First, decision training is not uniformly beneficial: Nimble-9B, which differs from Qwen3.5-9B only by a LoRA adapter, is better calibrated on intent tasks but 12.0 Macro-F1 points worse on financial sentiment. Second, under moderate shift Jev's expected calibration error (ECE) and threshold risk grow significantly more than a fine-tuned DeBERTa's only on financial sentiment, by 1.7 and 2.5 percentage points; averaged over 2,000 shift scenarios (exploratory), the gap is at most one point on every dataset. Third, EM prior correction with the model's mean development prediction as its prior needs no labels and lowers Jev's ECE under shift by 2.7 and 1.8 points on the two datasets where it is highest, although labelled recalibration lowers it far more. The three TDMs we tested are neither uniformly better than their backbones nor uniquely fragile under shift; for calibration, their main weakness is the miscalibration they already have without shift. We release all inputs, code and outputs.

摘要：

##### **StrucChunk: Structure-Aware Retrieval for Legal Document RAG**
2610.26313v1 by Surya Deep Singh, Ashutosh Tripathi, Gaurav Deep Singh

Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) can ground LLM outputs in domain-specific knowledge, but legal documents are hard: standard chunking fragments hierarchical argument structure, and generic embeddings struggle with both precise citation matching and semantic understanding. We propose StrucChunk (Structure Aware Chunking) with hybrid dense-sparse retrieval for hierarchically structured legal documents. It treats documents as directed acyclic graphs of provisions, preserves clause boundaries, resolves cross-references before embedding, and combines BM25 with BGE-M3 via Reciprocal Rank Fusion. We evaluate on five corpora, three legal traditions and two languages. StrucChunk reaches 100% boundary precision (vs. 7.7% for recursive splitting on CrPC), preserves 97.6% of documented cross-references (vs. 0%), and matches section coverage with 40.3% fewer chunks on GDPR. On LegalBench-RAG (6,858 query-passage pairs), it achieves Char-Recall@8 of 48.18% vs. 23.59% for the best baseline (+104%), surpassing the published state of the art by +83%. On 501 queries each for CrPC and CPC (Indian law), Recall@5 improves from 0.557 to 0.804 (+44.4%) and from 0.158 to 0.259 (+64.6%); chunking accounts for 88% and 73% of the gain. On 501 GDPR queries, Recall@5 is 0.868 with R@1 improving +59%. On NitiBench-CCL (3,730 Thai financial law queries), HR@5 is 0.856 vs. 0.594 for naive chunking (+44.1%), showing that section-boundary alignment is the dominant factor across languages, and cross-reference augmentation adds +2.3% on cross-reference queries. We release the implementation, 5,256 evaluation queries across five legal corpora, annotation methodology and chunking quality metrics. Code: github.com/SinghSuryaDeep/legal-rag-strucchunk-1

摘要：

##### **Selective Transfer through Sequence Distillation: From Benchmark Knowledge to Backdoor Behaviour**
2610.26311v1 by Jonibek Mansurov, Sholpan Bolatzhanova, Filbert Aurelian Tjiaranta, Vallerie Alexandara Putra, Alham Fikri Aji

Knowledge distillation is widely used to transfer capabilities from teacher models to student models, but recent work shows that it can also transfer unintended teacher properties. We study whether different hidden teacher properties survive the same distillation channels. Specifically, we compare two properties: benchmark-associated knowledge and refusal backdoors, and show that distillation treats them very differently. Teachers fine-tuned on benchmark data successfully pass that knowledge to students through all four distillation channels we test, including when students are trained only on teacher-generated number sequences containing no natural language or domain content whatsoever. Refusal backdoors tell the opposite story: backdoored teachers achieve near-perfect triggered refusal, yet sequence-distilled students inherit essentially none of this behaviour. Logit-based distillation transfers it substantially, reaching up to 85.5\% triggered refusal in students that saw zero backdoor examples. Distillation is therefore selective, and the selection criterion is not what data the student sees, but what structure the teacher's property has. The code is available at https://github.com/MJonibek/kd-leakage.

摘要：

##### **The Scheduler Is a Hidden Retriever: How Asynchrony Shapes Evidence Acquisition in Search Agents**
2610.26310v1 by Ruiyang Ren, Yuhao Wang, Yinan Zhang, Chunyan Miao

Language-model agents increasingly solve complex tasks through parallel reasoning and asynchronous information gathering. Controlled replay across two model families and three benchmarks shows that delivery timing redirects search decisions made before all results arrive, despite identical initial evidence. Acquisition disagreement reaches up to 24 percentage points above same-order repeat variation. This dependence persists after all initial evidence arrives and through a second search, turning temporary differences in what agents have seen into lasting differences in what they acquire. An acquisition-regret bound with a matching construction shows that invariance at a fixed early arrival count can limit the use of evidence already received. We introduce Confluent Evidence Acquisition (CEA), which instead releases local reasoning when each subquestion's evidence is complete and allocates shared search independently of completion order. For a fixed plan and stable conditional response law, we prove joint invariance of decisions, acquisitions, and answers. With question-only plans across three benchmarks, CEA brings acquisition disagreement to within 1.0 percentage point of repeat variation and achieves accuracy comparable to complete-input controls, improving over early commitment by up to 12.6 percentage points. Replacing CEA's fixed allocation with first-ready allocation under contention adds 21.6 percentage points of acquisition disagreement, while local query-target disagreement remains near repeat variation. Timed comparisons show that CEA reaches decisions earlier and responds faster than the same program waiting for all initial evidence.

摘要：

##### **Answering While You Type: Optimizing Time-to-Answer with Streaming Generation**
2610.26296v1 by Nikita Dragunov, Matvey Mikhalchuk, Anton Razzhigaev

Work on efficient large-language-model inference has concentrated almost entirely on reducing the cost of decoding once a request is complete. The quantity a user experiences, however, is the interval between submitting a request and receiving a usable response, and that interval can be shortened without making decoding itself faster. While a request is being composed it is already partially specified, yet conventional deployments begin no computation until submission. We propose and study streaming generation, a decoding regime in which the model begins producing a response from an incomplete request and revises it as the remaining input arrives, so that only a small residue of the answer remains to be computed once the request is submitted. We evaluate the regime on autoregressive and diffusion language models, characterise the conditions under which it reduces user-visible latency, and analyse the mechanism responsible.

摘要：

##### **Robotic Photography System based on Vision-Language-Action Model**
2610.26287v1 by Song-Li Wu, Chubin Zhang, Wenbo Ding, Yansong Tang

Modern robotic photography requires understanding photographic intent, planning camera motions, and producing aesthetically pleasing images in complex environments. We present RoboPhoto-VLA, a unified Vision-Language-Action framework that jointly models perception, reasoning, and camera control for autonomous robotic photography. RoboPhoto-VLA incorporates PhotoCoT for structured action planning and Aesthetic Direct Preference Optimization (ADPO) to align camera behaviors with human aesthetic preferences. To facilitate training and evaluation, we construct OpenPhotography, a robotic photography dataset with language-guided demonstrations. Extensive real-world experiments demonstrate that RoboPhoto-VLA produces photographs better aligned with human aesthetic preferences while generalizing effectively to unseen subjects, viewpoints, poses, and complex photographic instructions.

摘要：

##### **Physics-anchored residual quantum neural networks for cloud-cover parameterization**
2610.26279v1 by Zhuo Wang, Ce Yu, Bin Yang

Compact quantum neural networks have been explored for cloud-cover parameterization, but their predictive value depends on how physical knowledge is incorporated and which classical models provide the reference. We investigate an offline residual formulation that retains a fitted \XR\ cloud-cover estimate and learns a correction to its logit in transformed target space. Using released ICON/DYAMOND-derived data, we compare ten model variants in six- and eight-input settings, with three matched replicates and 10,000 training samples per replicate. All quantum circuits use local Z readout and noiseless exact expectation values. Under a common 30-epoch protocol, residual prediction reduced physical-space test mean squared error relative to direct QNN prediction by 54.3\% and 38.8\% for six-input XYZ and ZZXY circuits, and by 49.2\% and 38.9\% for their eight-input versions. Residual QNNs also outperformed the selected compact residual multilayer perceptron in every paired replicate. A larger, longer-trained Grundner-Q1 adaptation nevertheless achieved lower error: residual Q1 reached mean MSEs of 0.00778 and 0.00791, compared with 0.01124 and 0.01082 for residual XYZ. The results support residual prediction for compact simulated QNNs within the tested budget and establish a clear boundary set by stronger classical references. They do not establish computational quantum advantage or online climate-model skill.

#### Abstracts
##### **Intra-Rubric Reward Divergence: Mitigating Rubric Hacking through Latent Dimension Recovery and Balancing**
2610.26685v1 by Jianqiang Xia, Han Xu, Dianxi Shi

Rubric hacking limits both performance and training robustness in rubric-based reinforcement learning. By decomposing rubrics into sub-dimensions, we reveal the Intra-Rubric Reward Divergence: as the total rubric score increases, certain sub-dimension scores systematically decrease, suggesting the model sacrifices specific quality aspects to optimize the aggregated objective. From the sub-dimension perspective, we observe two phenomena: (1) dimension imbalance, causing the policy to favor heavily covered dimensions; (2) dimension absence, leading to a systematic gap between rewards and true quality. To mitigate these issues, we propose Fair-Race Dimension Aggregation (FRDA), which has generations compete within each dimension separately to mitigate rubric imbalance, and Residual Semantic Dimension Completion (RSDC), which mines evaluation dimensions implicitly encoded in a holistic preference signal to complete rubric coverage. Experiments in chat, science, and medical domains indicate that FRDA mitigates rubric hacking with more stable improvements, while RSDC outperforms both single-reward training and naive multi-reward aggregation, yielding generally higher-quality responses.

摘要：

##### **VaDar: Implicit Goal Conditioning via Value Disaggregation**
2610.26629v1 by Shashwat Saxena, Mehul Goel, Sreyas Venkataraman, Sarvesh Patil, Max Simchowitz

Long-horizon tasks are important for modern AI and robotics, yet pose a challenge for reinforcement learning. Historically, a popular approach has been to decompose a task into subgoals, and learn a subgoal-conditioned policy to execute each subtask in sequence.
  We introduce an alternative called Value Disaggregation (VaDar), which trains a single subgoal-independent policy to maximize a linear combination of per-subgoal critic values. Because subgoal information is needed only at training-time, VaDar removes the need for possibly costly or cumbersome subgoal-selection during policy deployment. Moreover, through careful experiments, we show that VaDar (1) is always competitive with, and frequently outperforms, natural baselines like goal-conditioning and (2) succeeds on tasks with non-sequential goal structure and multiple optimization objectives. In particular, VaDar is the first algorithm to solve challenging, long-horizon tasks in the RoboCasa suite, even when starting from a policy pretrained from behavior cloning with near-zero initial task success. Taken together, VaDar's successes suggest that subgoal information benefits actor-critic reinforcement learning by enhancing the efficacy of critic learning, whereas policy goal-conditioning is often unnecessary.

摘要：

##### **Mitigating Reward Hacking in Preference Reward Models with Evolving Penalties**
2610.26496v1 by Afonso Baldo, António Farinhas, Marcos Treviso, Ricardo Rei, Nuno M. Guerreiro

Reinforcement learning for language models has moved from preference reward models to verifiable rewards, which sustain much longer optimization. Preference models are prone to reward hacking: the policy raises their score without improving response quality, or even while degrading it. Yet for open-ended tasks, where quality can neither be verified nor fully written down as rubrics, they remain valuable. We introduce Dynalize, which mitigates reward hacking online, during policy training. Periodically and asynchronously, a language model analyzes training rollouts and writes penalties for undesirable behaviors that the reward model appears to reward; a judge applies them to every rollout. In clinical counseling and creative writing, training on the reward model alone raises its score continuously but ultimately leads to a decline in external benchmarks. Dynalize sustains longer training and results in improved downstream performance. For example, we find that our method suppresses patterns incentivized by preference models that, without penalties, spread to most responses, and even to the model's reasoning, which the reward model does not explicitly score. Furthermore, we show that its gains hold in multi-turn training, and even after we retrain the reward model on the discovered behaviors.

摘要：

##### **Autonomous intelligence for mining productivity: a multi-agent AI approach to operational value creation**
2610.26369v1 by N Guerin, A Dehoux, M Nakhla

Integrated Remote Operation Centres (IROCs) are emerging as the architectural frontier of Industry 4.0 for capital-intensive sectors such as mining, oil and gas, and energy. Yet two intertwined gaps constrain their evolution towards full autonomy. First, no integrated agentic architecture has been proposed for mining IROCs that orchestrates Supervisory Control and Data Acquisition systems, digital twins, predictive analytics and human-machine interfaces under safety-critical constraints. Second, no evaluation framework jointly addresses the real-time, Health-Safety-Environment, regulatory-auditability and cost-per-decision requirements that govern industrial agentic deployments. This paper makes four contributions. It synthesises industry needs and quantified gains documented in analogous industries that motivate an agentic approach to mining IROCs. It introduces EI2ROC, a three-tier multi-agent architecture combining a Large Language Model orchestrator with specialised monitoring and production-capacityprediction agents communicating through a Model Context Protocol-style tool interface. It provides a critical review of agentic evaluation along six orthogonal dimensions and identifies the industrial blind spot of current benchmarks. Finally, it proposes a multi-dimensional benchmarking framework for industrial agentic AI comprising scenario library, harness, metrics and humancalibration protocol. The paper states explicitly which components are implemented in the current prototype, which belong to the proposed reference design and which remain future work, and reports preliminary cost and latency observations from an operating site. Together, EI2ROC and the benchmarking framework constitute a methodological blueprint transferable across CAPEX intensive industries.

摘要：

##### **Fairness or Fallacy? Mapping the Landscape of AI Bias in Disease Detection**
2610.26215v1 by Ghulam Mudassir, Hameed Ullah Khan, Ahmad Sami Al-Shamayleh, Muhammad Faizan Khan, Muhammad Muqtasid, Moamin Abughazala, Adnan Akhunzada

Artificial intelligence (AI) has several transformative effects on healthcare, like to facilitate clinicians in disease detection and to manage patient care with enhanced precision. Due to the increasing complexity of medical data and the growing volume of diagnostic images, the substantial computational power needed to process such information creates both opportunities and challenges. To accomplish this objective, numerous methodologies have been proposed to integrate AI into disease detection systems. All these methodologies consider different aspects of clinical diagnosis, considering key parameters such as the type of AI model employed, the nature of input data, and the population demographics being analysed. In this paper, we review different state-of-the-art techniques which have been proposed for various areas of disease detection, specifically focusing on bias identification and mitigation. The proposed solutions are distinguished based on the type of input data they utilize, the architecture of model, and type of bias (algorithmic bias, demographic bias, model bias, and deployment bias). The time frame chosen to filter out relevant studies is between 2015 and 2026. Eventually, we review 23 related articles out of 64,389 analyzed from seven different digital libraries. The obtained results guide us to reach a conclusion with respect to state-of-the-art selected studies. Furthermore, these results reveal significant research gaps that can assist researchers in addressing AI-related bias in medical diagnosis. By prioritizing explainability, data diversity, and fairness in algorithmic design, as well as equitable access for affected patient populations, they can utilize available healthcare resources more effectively.

摘要：

##### **MoveBench: A Rubric-Based Benchmark for Evaluating Large Language Models in Sports and Health Coaching**
2610.26185v1 by Yitong Li, Jiang Bian, Jingling Wang, Pan Ding, Chengying Gu

Despite rapid advances in large language models (LLMs), their deployment in the sports and health domain remains high-risk due to the absence of domain-specific evaluation benchmarks. Existing general-purpose benchmarks (e.g., MMLU and HELM) primarily assess broad knowledge and reasoning capabilities, but fail to capture critical domain requirements such as exercise safety, physiological appropriateness, personalization, and actionable coaching quality. This gap is particularly concerning in fitness and health applications, where incorrect or unsafe recommendations can lead to physical harm. To address this limitation, we introduce MoveBench, the first comprehensive benchmarking framework specifically designed for evaluating LLMs in sports and health coaching. The proposed benchmark integrates heterogeneous task formats, including multiple-choice questions (MCQs) and open-ended question answering (OpenQA), grounded in real-world user scenarios and paired with structured rubrics emphasizing safety compliance, scientific validity, and training logic. Through large-scale evaluation of 15 diverse LLMs on 809 questions with 5,012 rubric-level criteria, our results reveal substantial performance variability, particularly in safety awareness, structured program design, and personalization capability, highlighting a critical gap between knowing and doing. Our work establishes a foundational evaluation standard for sports and health LLM applications and provides a reproducible framework for advancing safe, reliable, and context-aware AI-driven coaching systems. Open resources are available at https://github.com/SatoSakula/MoveBench.

摘要：

##### **How Robust Are Prognostic Radiomic Features? Comparing Selection Techniques on Pooled Glioblastoma Cohorts**
2610.26095v1 by Yannick Sodonougbo, Marlene Schnieders, Alexia Rizoudis, Santiago Cepeda, Frank Kramer, Dominik Müller

Radiomic survival studies in glioblastoma report heterogeneous prognostic features, but how much of this stems from the methodology itself is rarely examined. We analysed the association of 463 radiomic, anatomical and clinical features from pre-treatment MRI with overall survival in 705 IDH-wildtype glioblastoma patients from three public cohorts, using three techniques: a log-rank test after median split, univariate Cox regression (both Benjamini-Hochberg corrected) and an XGBoost regressor interpreted with SHAP. The log-rank test selected 99 features, Cox regression 72 and SHAP its top 20; five were selected by all three: age, a peritumoural T2 texture feature, the number of connected peritumoural mask components and two image-level intensity maxima. The two survival tests shared 51 features, about half of the SHAP top 20 were also selected by either test, and where selections overlapped, effect directions agreed. Many selections were PyRadiomics diagnostic descriptors that are not restricted to tumour voxels. Analytical pipeline choices, including ranking method, pre-filtering and descriptor handling, can substantially influence which features appear prognostic.

摘要：

##### **CODA: Cohort- and Drift-aware Foundation Model for Multimodal Clinical Reasoning**
2610.25871v1 by Changshuo Liu, Jiaqi Zhu, Wenqiao Zhang, Xiaokui Xiao, Beng Chin Ooi

Recent advances in multimodal foundation models have demonstrated strong performance across diverse clinical tasks. However, existing approaches predominantly operate at the individual sample level and fail to model the interplay between population-level cohort patterns and patient-specific variation that underlie real-world clinical reasoning. In practice, clinicians routinely reason through patient cohorts sharing similar demographic, physiological, or pathological characteristics, while accounting for patient-specific deviations from the group prototype. Motivated by this gap, we propose CODA, a cohort- and drift-aware foundation model for multimodal clinical reasoning. CODA explicitly integrates cohort structure into the modeling pipeline through two complementary mechanisms: cohort-aware population stratification and drift-aware individual adaptation. In particular, we leverage reinforcement learning to discover clinically meaningful patient cohorts, and further model each patient's deviation from cohort prototypes as a structured drift signal encoding fine-grained individual heterogeneity. These cohort- and drift-aware representations are incorporated into the foundation model to enable reasoning that is simultaneously informed by population-level patterns and sensitive to individual-level variation. Extensive experiments on real-world electronic health record (EHR) benchmarks demonstrate that CODA achieves consistent effectiveness and strong generalizability across diverse clinical tasks, including closed-QA, open-QA, and report generation.

摘要：

##### **When to Stop Asking: Factor Coverage Bias and LLM Determinability Judgments with Underspecified Information in Medicine**
2610.25819v1 by Yusuke Watanabe, Yohei Kobashi, Taisei Tosaki, Takeshi Kojima, Yusuke Iwasawa, Yasushi Okuno, Yutaka Matsuo

Clinical decision-making often begins with underspecified information and requires not only gathering missing information but also knowing when to stop. This calls for judging determinability (whether the available information is sufficient for a decision) by considering whether missing information could change the conclusion, not simply the amount of available decision-relevant information. Reliance on this amount as a cue for determinability, which we term \textit{factor coverage bias}, could undermine the trustworthiness of large language models (LLMs) through premature conclusions or excessive information gathering, such as unnecessary invasive tests. Yet whether LLMs exhibit this bias remains insufficiently studied. To address this gap, we construct a controlled pairwise benchmark in which LLMs identify the determinable case within each determinable--undeterminable pair. We add a decision-relevant factor to either side while keeping each case's determinability unchanged, and similarly add irrelevant text to distinguish this bias from context-length effects. Adding a relevant factor made LLMs more likely to select the augmented case as determinable even when it remained undeterminable, an effect that persisted with increased reasoning effort, while adding irrelevant text made them less likely to select the augmented case, an effect that reasoning substantially reduced. These findings identify factor coverage bias as a failure mode distinct from context-length effects and a potential risk to LLM trustworthiness. Our work provides an evaluation framework for LLM trustworthiness under underspecification.

摘要：

##### **AirMPA: A Meteorology-to-Pollution Adapter for Global Air Quality Forecasting**
2610.25797v1 by Yiheng Wang, Kai Zheng, Yuetan Lin, Fanglu Fan, Chenliang Tao, Guochao Chen, Hongliang Zhang, Hao Li

Accurate real-time forecasting of atmospheric pollutants is essential for reducing exposure risks and improving public health. Traditional numerical forecasting systems suffer from incomplete parameterizations, uncertain inputs, and high computational cost. Existing deep learning approaches improve efficiency, but often entangle meteorological and pollutant variables within unified forecasting frameworks, limiting flexibility and pollutant-specific representation learning. To address these challenges, we propose AirMPA, a decoupled autoregressive framework for meteorology-to-pollution forecasting. AirMPA predicts 13 atmospheric pollutants at 0.4° spatial resolution and 12-h temporal resolution using meteorological fields, emission priors, and historical pollutant concentrations. The model is built on an improved Swin-Transformer architecture with zonal release attention and dual-branch fusion, enabling more effective modeling of global transport continuity and heterogeneous pollutant dynamics. During training, AirMPA uses the fifth-generation atmospheric reanalysis from the European Centre for Medium-Range Weather Forecasts (ERA5) as meteorological forcing, while during inference it directly ingests forecast fields from external meteorological models, enabling stable autoregressive prediction up to 5 days ahead. Experiments show that AirMPA consistently outperforms Aurora and surpasses the Copernicus Atmosphere Monitoring Service (CAMS) in approximately 90% of pollutant-lead-time evaluation cases over the 5-day forecast horizon, while outperforming CAMS on all variables beyond 48 h. These results demonstrate the potential of decoupled meteorology-to-pollution forecasting for global air quality prediction.

摘要：

##### **TRACE: Tracking Multimodal Clinical Trajectories for Inpatient Risk Prediction**
2610.25765v1 by Minjun Kim, Jong Hak Moon

Accurate inpatient risk prediction requires modeling heterogeneous clinical evidence that accumulates irregularly over time. This challenge spans in-hospital mortality, prolonged length of stay, and readmission, each with task-specific observation windows. Existing multimodal approaches combine structured health record data, chest x-rays, and clinical text, but primarily focus on modality fusion or missing-modality compensation, with limited attention to temporal trajectories over an admission. We propose TRACE (TRAjectory modeling of multimodal Clinical Events), a daily-scale framework organizing clinical events into day-level trajectories, separating day-invariant global context from day-specific local events drawn from EHR data, chest x-rays, radiology reports, and discharge summaries. The trajectory is encoded using temporal attention, allowing unavailable modalities to emit no tokens. A phenotype-guided contrastive objective further organizes admissions under heterogeneous disease distributions. Across cohorts from MIMIC-IV, eICU, and MIMIC-III comprising nearly 180,000 total stays, we evaluate TRACE on mortality, length of stay, and readmission prediction. TRACE achieves the highest AUROC across all nine dataset-task settings and the highest ACC in eight, outperforming baselines. Ablation studies validate our attention masks and contrastive learning, while trajectory-level attribution localizes risk-associated hospital days and modalities.

摘要：

##### **A Calibrated Instrument for Detecting Disposition Drift in Language Models**
2610.25735v1 by Jerry Kaplan

Before language models are released, they are tested for harmful traits such as willingness to carry out dangerous requests, deception, power seeking, and sycophancy. The safe behavior these tests certify may degrade as models are modified, by topical fine-tuning, new training data, or changes to settings such as the depth of their reasoning. Most work reports such drift as single summary rates scored by another AI model, without the error analysis needed to interpret them. We adapt a calibrated measurement methodology recently developed for assessing how inference optimizations affect the quality of model outputs. Applying it to several misalignment dimensions in two open-weight models, we first establish the instrument's precision, showing that shifts of a few percentage points can be reliably detected. We then induce drift deliberately by fine-tuning on correct and on subtly harmful medical and financial advice; harmful advice induces a far greater shift than correct advice. Most strikingly, a model that reasons before answering can conceal the shift almost entirely in its answers, even though a direct reading of its internal state shows that its disposition has moved. With reasoning switched off, the same model shifts steadily as the share of harmful advice in its training grows. These results demonstrate the importance of testing safety with reasoning turned both on and off.

摘要：

##### **PFedQFT: Personalized Federated Quantum-Inspired Fine-Tuning for Medical Large Language Models**
2610.25712v1 by Qihan Huang, Lingxia Zhang, Guye Zhang, Zizhu Wang, Xiaowei Mao

Medical data remain fragmented across institutional silos because of privacy, governance, and ownership constraints. Federated learning enables medical institutions to adapt large language models (LLMs) collaboratively without transferring raw records. However, independently parameterized low-rank adapters incur repeated parameter and communication costs across Transformer projections, while uniform aggregation may limit adaptation to heterogeneous institutional data. We propose PFedQFT, a quantum-inspired framework for personalized federated fine-tuning with a compact shared adapter. Specifically, PFedQFT constructs reusable orthonormal adaptation bases from compact skew-symmetric generators using a truncated matrix-exponential approximation followed by QR orthonormalization. Compatible projections reuse these bases, while a globally shared full core preserves the flexibility of each projection. Each client further maintains a lightweight private residual operator, whose directions are learned globally and whose coefficients remain local. Experiments on seven public medical tasks and multiple LLM backbones show substantial reductions in trainable parameters and per-round adapter upload volume, with competitive predictive performance on most tasks.

摘要：

##### **ACorD: Adaptive Correspondence Discovery for Medical Image Registration**
2610.25638v1 by Arghavan Rezvani, Kun Han, Xiaohui Xie

Medical image registration is a fundamental task for aligning anatomical structures across images. Exhaustively searching for point-wise correspondences is prohibitively expensive in both computation and memory, particularly for high-resolution 3D medical images. Most recent learning-based methods directly predict correspondences in a coarse-to-fine manner, but often propagate intermediate estimates across scales without explicitly weighting correspondence evidence, which can allow coarse-scale errors to persist during refinement. To address these challenges, we propose ACorD, an adaptive correspondence discovery framework designed to mitigate error propagation with an attention-guided process. ACorD first predicts multiple potential correspondence candidates through Correlation-guided Cross-scale Offset Prediction (CCOP), which combines correlation maps as spatial matching priors with cross-scale features as global deformation context. Then, Multi-Head Deformable Aggregation (MHDA) aggregates these candidate correspondences using attention, producing a learned, non-uniform weighting over competing matches rather than treating them equally. The multi-head design further captures complementary deformation patterns, ensuring both global alignment and local precision. By integrating adaptive offset prediction with selective, attention-based aggregation of correspondence evidence, ACorD enables accurate deformation refinement. Extensive experiments on four public medical registration benchmarks demonstrate that adaptive correspondence discovery within a multi-scale refinement framework improves registration robustness across diverse anatomical structures.

摘要：

##### **MapSteal: Map-based Autonomous Knowledge Stealing Attack Against Large Language Models**
2610.25626v1 by Zhengao Li, Chuxu Zhang, Yushun Dong

Knowledge acquisition from black-box large language models (LLMs) offers a practical approach to adapting smaller student models. However, many existing methods rely on predefined queries or student feedback to guide acquisition, leaving autonomous expansion of knowledge targets an important challenge. We propose \textbf{MapSteal}, a student-independent framework for structured recursive knowledge acquisition. Starting from a small set of initial roots, MapSteal represents acquisition targets using structured states and recursively expands them through teacher-proposed directions. Question realization, proposal admission, and deduplication constrain the acquisition process, while root-aware scheduling organizes exploration under a fixed QA budget. The resulting question--answer pairs are used to adapt student models without requiring student feedback during acquisition. Experiments with Qwen3.5 students at 2B, 4B, and 9B scales show competitive downstream performance across six medical and cybersecurity benchmarks. On the 4B medical evaluation, Full MapSteal improves average accuracy by 1.17 percentage points over a simplified recursive acquisition variant. Cross-domain evaluations show that gains depend on the domain and evaluation panel. These results demonstrate the feasibility of structured, student-independent recursive acquisition while highlighting the distinction between generating acquisition targets and obtaining useful training supervision. Our code is available at \href{https://github.com/LabRAI/Mapsteal} {https://github.com/LabRAI/Mapsteal}.

##### **When Better Opportunities Backfire: Service Worker Responses to Parallel AI Deployment in Customer Acquisition**
2610.25603v1 by Xinyu Fu, Shulong Luo, Narayan Ramasubbu, Peng Shen, Xincheng Wang, Yucheng Wang

As AI voice agents get deployed in high-volume contact centers, firms can route low-intent leads to AI and allocate high-intent leads to human workers. Drawing on sequential search theory, we posit that AI-induced task allocation reshapes work, enriching opportunities facing human workers but also raising their reservation value for continuing with the lead in hand, making them less willing to persist. We study the effects of an AI voice-agent deployment at a Chinese online health-insurance brokerage using more than a half million customer outreach records surrounding the rollout. Adopting a regression-discontinuity-in-time design, we trace how worker behavior changes across the workday. Results show that the enrichment of human-facing opportunities concentrates early in the workday, raising the probability that a call is a high-intent lead by about 23 percentage points on the first three calls and fading to zero later. However, customer acquisition by human workers declines precisely where the enrichment is greatest, falling about 5 percentage points on the first three calls and flattening over the day. Consistent with the theorized reservation value mechanism, workers reallocate their earliest leads about 8 percentage points more often and persist less with them, shortening those calls by about 13%. A calibrated decomposition analysis further shows that the modest gain from improved lead ordering is outweighed by a much larger negative behavioral response. By reshaping the quality and within-workday distribution of opportunities, parallel AI deployment alters how workers pursue tasks, highlighting opportunity distribution as an important service operations state variable beyond workload and congestion.

摘要：

##### **Design and Cross-Domain Evaluation of an Agent Execution Harness**
2610.25568v1 by Saurav Joshi, Manas Gupta, Ashwin Venkatesh Raman, Akshay Budhkar

Completing complex tasks with language-model agents requires coordinating planning, execution, and review so the final result addresses the user's original objective. We present an agent execution harness that keeps the original objective separate from a revisable plan and an execution record. A planner assigns scoped tasks, workers produce outputs and artifacts, and a reviewer examines results against the original request before submitting or requesting additional work. Reusable domain-specific instructions guide these roles and are refined during construction using task inputs and execution trajectories, without official grading feedback. We evaluate complete harness configurations across terminal tasks, clinical responses, application building, and scientific questions. On Terminal-Bench 2.1, the harness passes 83 of 89 instances (93.26%); on HealthBench Professional, a Fable-configured system achieves a length-adjusted rubric score of 69.63 across 525 tasks. In a 53-task paired HealthBench comparison, the Fable-configured harness achieves an aggregate score 19.89 points higher than direct completion, despite a $-1.46$-point median task-level change; mean inference cost rises from \$0.29 to \$2.13 per task. We combine these outcomes with latency, documented instruction revisions, and reviewer decisions. Evaluating outcomes alongside execution trajectories and resource use clarifies how coordination supports task completion, where it introduces regressions, and at what cost.

##### **From Clinical Risk Prediction to Operational Decisions: An Uncertainty-Aware Framework for ICU Staffing**
2610.25530v1 by Jiayan Yang, Ad Feelders, Arno Siebes

Advances in electronic health records (EHR) and large-scale critical care databases have enabled the development of machine learning models for the early detection of patient deterioration. While these models often achieve strong predictive performance for outcomes such as sepsis and mortality, most studies evaluate them purely on discrimination metrics. This leaves a critical gap: how to systematically translate probabilistic clinical risk estimates into downstream operational decisions under real-world constraints, such as shift rigidity and hiring delays.
  This study proposes an uncertainty-aware anticipatory staffing framework that bridges short-term patient risk prediction and staff planning without altering the upstream predictive model. Instead of relying on naive deterministic aggregation or fixed safety buffers, the framework explicitly models dynamic predictive uncertainty and evaluates extreme shortage exposure using Conditional Value-at-Risk (CVaR). These aggregated, risk-calibrated demand signals are then embedded into an hourly rolling-horizon scheduling policy to generate delay-constrained staffing decisions.
  Evaluated on realistic trajectories from the MIMIC-IV database, the proposed framework demonstrates a favorable cost-shortage trade-off compared to deterministic and purely reactive baselines. Pareto frontier analysis illustrates how uncertainty-aware demand construction changes resource allocation under varying safety preferences, while stress testing confirms the framework's operational robustness even when subjected to systematic predictive bias in the form of calibration drift. By formalizing this translation layer, this work provides a principled, transparent pathway for safely integrating probabilistic AI outputs into high-stakes healthcare operational systems.

摘要：

##### **Multimodal-Conditioned Latent Diffusion Models for Spatiotemporal Brain MRI Forecasting**
2610.25481v1 by Antonio Scardace, Silvia Ingala, Sebastiano Battiato, Daniele Ravì

Spatiotemporal forecasting of 3D anatomical changes is a critical generative vision challenge for modeling Alzheimer's Disease (AD) progression. While brain MRI forecasting from single-modality data has advanced, other key complementary multimodal biomarkers remain underutilized. In this work, we propose the Multimodal-Conditioned Latent Diffusion Model (MC-LDM), a novel framework for patient-specific brain MRI synthesis. MC-LDM leverages multimodal biomarkers---MRI, tau-PET, Clinical Dementia Rating (CDR), and APOE4---during training, but relies only on baseline MRI, APOE4, and patient metadata at inference, reflecting realistic clinical availability. Our contributions are threefold: (1) a Latent Diffusion Model (LDM) fine-tuned via Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) for data-efficient learning; (2) a multimodal conditioning that integrates molecular and clinical signals to model heterogeneous disease trajectories; and (3) auxiliary predictors estimating unobserved multimodal biomarkers from MRI features and patient covariates to condition the diffusion model, removing the need for these assessments at inference. Trained on 1,355 visits from 852 subjects in the Alzheimer's Disease Neuroimaging Initiative (ADNI), MC-LDM reduces average regional volumetric MAE by 18% over the second-best baseline on an independent test set of 664 visits. To our knowledge, MC-LDM represents the first diffusion-based framework to leverage multimodal priors for MRI forecasting. This conceptual advancement enables patient-specific trajectory generation that captures aspects of AD heterogeneity not observable from structural anatomy alone, bridging the gap between rich multimodal supervision and the strict constraints of routine clinical deployment. Code is available at: https://github.com/antonioscardace/MC-LDM.

摘要：

##### **Reliability-Preserving Compression for Deployable Medical Image Segmentation**
2610.25460v1 by Sagar Kumar, Shailja Sharma, Tirthajit Baruah, Punit Rathore

Post-training compression of pretrained medical segmentation models commonly uses quantization, pruning, or distillation to reduce model size and computational cost. Existing medical segmentation quantization studies mainly evaluate Dice, model size, memory, and latency, but this is incomplete for safety-critical use because compressed models should also preserve uncertainty signals that indicate unreliable predictions. We study this problem in compressed MRI/CT segmentation and formalize why Dice can remain stable while uncertainty changes. Motivated by this failure mode, we propose score-guided aggregate-validated mixed precision (SGAV-MP), a post-training compression framework that selects layer precisions under reliability constraints. SGAV-MP ranks quantization actions with a compact benefit-risk score and accepts them only after full-model reliability validation on a calibration set. Across five 2D backbones and three 3D benchmarks, SGAV-MP preserves FP32-level Dice and high uncertainty-map agreement, achieving $3.59\times$--$7.8\times$ compression in 2D and $4.73\times$--$7.82\times$ in 3D. We also report backend-supported TensorRT deployment measurements separately to distinguish runtime execution from reliability-preserving storage compression.

##### **ViCoR: Estimating Visual Necessity via Counterfactual Residuals for Multimodal Medical Data Selection**
2610.25202v1 by Haotian Gan, Yudong Li, Xintian Li, Jiarui Han, Weidong Tang

Medical vision-language models often exhibit hidden stratification, where high overall performance masks failures on subtle visual findings and overlapping clinical conditions. We trace this failure mode to modality imbalance during fine-tuning, where strong linguistic priors can suppress visually grounded learning and induce plausible but weakly grounded rationales. Existing data selection methods usually score image-text pairs as holistic units, without separating visually necessary samples from shortcut-solvable ones. To address this problem, we propose ViCoR, a counterfactual-residual-based data selection method for Med-VLM fine-tuning. ViCoR estimates visual necessity through image-dependent optimization residuals, calibrates the resulting scores with medical supervision compatibility, and constructs a diversity-aware fine-tuning subset. Across two backbones, four external generalization benchmarks, and one multi-disease robustness benchmark, ViCoR-selected 20\% subsets achieve the strongest average performance compared with full-data fine-tuning and selection baselines. Gradient-level analysis and perturbation audits further show that ViCoR enriches image-dependent training signals and strengthens image-level and evidence-region dependence. The code will be available at https://github.com/htgan-ai/ViCoR.

##### **ClinSDT: LLM-Encoded Clinical Semantic Guidance for Sepsis Treatment via Decision Transformer**
2610.25121v1 by Yanyan Wu, Guanyu Zhu, Changbin Zhao, Jin Zhang, Dexu Yu, Tianmin Sun, Jining Luan, Chunxiao Li, Hanwen Du, Youhua Li

Offline sepsis treatment aims to learn treatment policies from retrospective ICU trajectories. Decision Transformer-based methods provide a natural sequence modeling framework for this task, but they mainly rely on raw physiological states and historical clinician actions. Such raw trajectory tokens may not explicitly capture clinical semantics that clinicians use in practice, such as patient severity, recent treatment response, and current risk priorities. To address this limitation, we propose ClinSDT, an LLM-assisted Decision Transformer framework for offline sepsis treatment. ClinSDT uses a frozen LLM as a semantic bridge rather than a treatment decision maker. It constructs three clinical semantic views from ICU trajectories, namely Profile, Context, and Advisory, and inserts their encoded representations as independent tokens into a Decision Transformer policy. To support policy refinement and rollout, ClinSDT further trains a Treatment-Response Forecaster to estimate the next physiological state, SAPS-II change, and predictive uncertainty from state-action inputs. In the second training stage, the forecaster screens local counterfactual treatment candidates, and only low-uncertainty candidates with lower predicted SAPS-II increase are distilled into the policy after semantic regeneration. During inference, ClinSDT performs closed-loop treatment rollout by refreshing semantic inputs from the currently available patient state. Experiments on MIMIC-III and MIMIC-IV show that ClinSDT achieves consistent relative improvements over representative imitation learning, offline reinforcement learning, and Decision Transformer baselines under the same rollout protocol. Code and data are available at https://github.com/KAG778/ClinSDT.

##### **Auto-MD-bench: Evaluating Autoresearch Agents for Real-World Medical Tasks**
2610.25069v1 by Shenqi Zhou, Qiran Zou, Wenhao Zhao, Zhenyu Liao, Dianbo Liu

Autonomous agents are increasingly capable of developing machine learning (ML) models end to end. In medical practice, however, strong performance on the target metric does not fully characterize how good the models are in real-world use cases. For example, medical ML models should also provide well-calibrated risk estimates and remain robust across patient populations. Yet autonomous model development is typically guided by task objectives that emphasize simple predictive discrimination (e.g. AUROC). This raises a key question: are these objectives sufficient to guide agents toward models that meet the broader requirements of real-world medical use? In this study, we introduce Auto-MD-bench, a benchmark of 15 real-world medical tasks from 10 clinical datasets, spanning survival analysis, classification and sequential prediction, with external validation across cohorts, hospitals and time periods. We evaluate four agents: AIDE, AI Scientist v2, Codex, and Autoresearch. The strongest agents achieve competitive primary scores, yet their predicted risks are less calibrated than those of the simple baseline models, which could lead to severe medical accidents. Moreover, models with nearly identical primary scores can exhibit markedly different calibration, while performance varies substantially across population shifts. These findings show that primary task performance alone is insufficient to assess autonomous clinical modeling and multiple different metrics widely used in healthcare practice need to be taken into consideration.

##### **Label-Space Mismatch Distorts Cross-Cohort Evaluation in Breast Cancer Molecular Subtyping**
2610.24962v1 by Suxing Liu, Byungwon Min, Zhijian Zheng, Jiahao Wang

Cross-cohort evaluation of breast cancer subtype classifiers is unreliable when source and target cohorts use different annotation standards: METABRIC labels six classes, PAM50 defines five, and a claudin-low prediction cannot be correct under a five-class standard. A classifier transferred from 1,369 METABRIC patients (train n=958) to TCGA-BRCA (n=987) and GSE21653 (n=266) separates three evaluation operations routinely conflated in cross-site comparisons. (1) Label-space restriction - an evaluation-rule change, not a retraining gain - is the dominant correction (73.76%->79.74% on TCGA-BRCA; 63.16%->68.42% on GSE21653); (2) EM prior correction on the reconciled space is an optional calibration step (Brier fidelity up, macro F1 down; to 80.65% and 74.06%); and (3) the target label space is not recoverable from unlabelled predictions (EM retains prior 0.0751 on the absent class; calibrated detectors fail), so compliance must come from cohort documentation. The protocol exposes why internal validation overstates cross-cohort value: multimodal fusion gains do not transfer (no configuration beats mRNA alone, n=694; McNemar p=0.13-1.00), and the genomic panel adds nothing to pathological complete response prediction over clinical variables (DeltaAUC = +0.0045). Clinically, without label-space compliance, cross-site accuracy comparisons misattribute annotation-standard differences to model skill; Luminal A/B and claudin-low boundary errors map to distinct treatment decisions; and subtype accuracy is a weaker portable endpoint than survival discrimination (external C-index 0.577-0.584). Reporting the cohort's allowed label set and applying label-space compliance are therefore prerequisites before any cross-cohort performance claim.

##### **CasePlay: Self-Play Reinforcement Learning from Case Reports for Medical Reasoning**
2610.24952v1 by Hao Wang, Zihan Wang, Kai Wu, Jian Yao, Yiwen Ye, Yongcan Luo, Dapeng Wu, Jiale Chen, Haihua Yang

Reinforcement learning (RL) is a promising way to improve the clinical reasoning abilities of large language models (LLMs), but current pipelines often rely on expert-labeled questions or costly synthetic-data generation. Meanwhile, the medical literature offers a wealth of high-quality unlabeled documents, including clinical case reports that trace the full clinical course from initial presentation to final treatment decisions. We study whether document-grounded self-play can convert these reports into effective RL training signals. We identify two challenges that limit existing self-play methods in this setting: knowledge-point fixation}, where the proposer repeatedly asks about a narrow set of salient clues in a source report, and difficulty-quality mismatch, where appropriately difficult questions may still be poorly formed or shallow, limiting their value for reasoner training. To address these challenges, we propose CasePlay, a self-play framework with two key components: (a) a Knowledge-Conditioned Proposer, which anchors question generation to case-specific knowledge points so that questions draw on a broader range of evidence from the source report; and (b) a Rubric-Judge Reasoner, which augments answer-accuracy feedback with rubric-based quality feedback on clue integration, reasoning depth, and option quality, guiding the proposer toward reasoning-intensive questions that better support reasoner training. Extensive experiments across seven medically related benchmarks demonstrate that CasePlay outperforms existing self-play baselines and improves the SFT warm-start model by 3.1 points on average, highlighted by a 6.3-point gain on LiveClin. This approach offers an effective path toward continually improving medical reasoning ability through self-play RL.

##### **Medical Fine-Tuning Reshapes, Not Reduces, Patient-Facing AI Risk: A Rubric-Based, LLM-Judge Evaluation Framework**
2610.24807v1 by Savan Doshi

Patient-facing medical question answering with large language models (LLMs) is moving from research demonstrations to production deployments, yet evaluation remains anchored on factual correctness. Factuality is necessary but not sufficient: a response can be factually grounded and still issue a directive that, if acted on, would harm the patient. We introduce a risk-sensitive evaluation framework that scores responses by the presence and severity of six categories of risk-bearing language (treatment directives, contraindications, dosage expressions, urgency-triage cues, high-alert medication mentions, and overconfident assertions), anchored on published clinical harm taxonomies. Severity is classified by a three-judge LLM ensemble. We apply this framework to six 7-9B instruction-tuned models, three general (Mistral-7B, Phi-3.5-mini, Llama-3.1-8B) and three medically adapted (BioMistral-7B, OpenBioLLM-Llama3-8B, Med42-Llama3-8B), on 200 real patient questions from the K-QA benchmark. Two findings emerge. First, ensemble risk-bearing language is statistically independent of factual hallucination rate (pooled Spearman $ρ= -0.065$, $n = 300$, 95% CI $[-0.18, +0.05]$; distance correlation $= 0.10$, independence test $p = 0.24$). Second, medical fine-tuning does not reduce aggregate risk but reshapes it: continued-pretraining adaptations (BioMistral, OpenBioLLM) consistently suppress high-alert medication mentions while amplifying overconfident assertions, whereas clinical instruction-tuning (Med42) suppresses dosing only, without amplifying overconfidence. Per-category Krippendorff $α$ is fair to good for the four linguistically anchored categories (0.53-0.71) and low for the two clinical-judgment-dependent categories (0.19, 0.31), indicating where automatic safety evaluation is and is not yet reliable. We release the rubric, judge prompts, and evaluation code.

##### **Evidence Field: Adaptive Covering Programs for Medical Imaging**
2610.24773v1 by Isarar Siddique, Raiyan Siddique, Arshi Niyaz, Jamyang Ugyen Tshomo, Atish Raza

Medical image classifiers typically return predictions without a native contract for independently reconstructing their spatial decision computation. Evidence Field is a trainable spatial-program architecture that jointly produces class scores, selected covering programs and signed evidence. Its encoder combines absolute and image-relative response fields with associative spatial moments and learned characteristic sums. A typed classifier scores individual regions and parent-conditioned multiplicative groups; a covering recursion selects scale, partition and composition. We establish exact one-fold coverage, optimality within the registered grammar, a bound relating smooth training to hard inference, signed-score conservation and class-centred offset invariance. Direct contractions and their adjoint specify arithmetic and storage scaling on two- and three-dimensional grids. Native output records bind inputs, parameters, supports, types and contextual dependencies to individual and ensemble predictions. In a separately reported histopathology application, patient-separated training and evaluation across held-out centres provide empirical evidence for the architecture. Trained mechanism and corrected-loss comparisons distinguish predictive effects from implementation correctness; independent readers reconstruct descriptors, routes, scores and probability mixtures. The principal contribution is a coupled representation, decision and evidence architecture whose selected computation can be reproduced independently.

##### **From Prediction to Action: Population Health World Model for Digital Medicine**
2610.24662v1 by Xike Xie, Yu Li, Weizhong Yang, Amalie Dyda, Shaohua Kevin Zhou

Digital medicine now captures data that traces individual health trajectories and reveals population health dynamics through electronic health records, claims, remote monitoring, wearable devices, mobile devices, social sensing data, medical images, etc. But much of its modeling still centers on task-specific, outcome-specific, and prediction-specific estimation. The next frontier is not simply better prediction, but action in an evolving health world shaped by multiple decision-makers, heterogeneous populations, resource constraints, and feedback in care delivery. This is why digital medicine now needs world models: not only to estimate what will happen, but to represent how actions change what can happen next -- whom to prioritize, how to allocate scarce resources, how to adapt care across subgroups, and how interventions reshape future health trajectories and population dynamics.
  We call such a model population health world model (PHWM): a more foundational representation of an evolving health world in which health states, individual trajectories, population health dynamics, interventions, constraints, and feedback are intrinsically coupled. Building a PHWM endows three unmet structural gaps in current digital medicine: dynamic health-state modeling, population-level intervention intelligence, and validation, equity, and governance for action-oriented use. We argue that addressing these gaps requires three minimal capabilities: representation learning under action-dependent observation, multi-scale intervention intelligence, and validation with explicit decision boundaries, while highlighting the importance of social, contextual, and public-health sensing data for action-oriented digital medicine.

##### **Concordance Plausibility Check for Intervention-Conditioned Human Digital Twins**
2610.24577v1 by Saeed Farzi, Christopher Kipps, John McBeth, Gabriela Czanner, Sofia Michopoulou, Benjamin Macarthur, Sarah Ennis, Paul Elkington, Nicholas C Harvey, Rahman Attar

Intervention-conditioned human digital twins predict treatment alternatives that cannot be observed in the same patient. Ordinary predictive accuracy therefore cannot establish counterfactual credibility. We propose the Concordance Plausibility Check (CPC), a claim-centered framework combining real-world evidence, qualified simulation and clinical knowledge. CPC matches evidence to the causal claim, audits contradictions and shared dependencies, and reports Pass, Fail or reason-coded Indeterminate.

##### **ASR Personalization in Pathological Voice: Accuracy, Efficiency, and Duration Effects**
2610.24572v1 by Giuseppe Giaimis, Sergei Katkov, Muhammad Azfar Yaqub, Antonio Liotta

Automatic Speech recognition (ASR) struggles with Pathological speech due to persistent speaker-specific mismatch. In clinical and accessibility scenarios, only limited target-speaker data is typically available, making full model retraining impractical. We present a component-level analysis of LoRA adaptation in Whisper, investigating how adapter placement interacts with the model size, training efficiency and utterance-duration in single-speaker settings. Our experimental comparative study shows that while scaling reduces errors, it does not eliminate mismatch. Furthermore, the full attention LoRa provides the most reliable accuracy, and the best time--accuracy trade-off. Improvements vary across utterance length, with medium-length recordings benefiting the most, while short and long utterances remain challenging. These findings highlight the importance of adaptation placement and duration-dependent evaluation, under challenging, data-scarce personalization scenarios.

##### **Trust the Edge, Doubt the Arrow: Auditing Validation Signals for Text-Mined Clinical Causal Knowledge Graphs**
2610.24390v1 by Su Lan, Shicheng Zhou, Yiqi Wang, Yanming Zhu, Xuefei Yin, Alan Wee-Chung Liew

Clinical causal retrieval-augmented generation systems increasingly use knowledge graphs (KGs) mined from notes, yet extracted assertions remain candidates that require audit for patient-level EHR support and direction. We construct a directed candidate graph from 328,839 MIMIC-IV discharge summaries using LLM extraction and UMLS normalization, and evaluate three signals against SemMedDB: within-admission co-occurrence lift, cross-admission diagnosis-coding temporal precedence, and literature corroboration. Lift filters unsupported co-mentions and provides useful EHR support, but not causal proof. Coding order is a weak direction signal: among the 686 candidates where it commits, it reaches 54.2% agreement with SemMedDB, below the extractor's 69.4% on the same subset. Literature corroboration supplies independent direction evidence but covers only 30.4% of candidate relations. A MIMIC-III replication yields the same pattern: extractor direction remains stable (70.5%), whereas coding-order direction is near chance (50.9%). Thus, text-mined clinical causal candidate KGs are best treated as EHR-supported but direction-uncertain. In a leakage-controlled causal QA benchmark, lift-only retrieval achieves 35.7% accuracy; conservative causal demotion achieves 37.2% while reducing evidence-unsupported answers from 17.3% to 6.1% at fixed retrieval recall. We release code, aggregate summaries, and scripts to regenerate restricted outputs.

##### **Screening LLM Judges Without Ground Truth: A Preliminary Evaluation of an AI Dental Hygiene Coach**
2610.24335v1 by William DeShong, Barath Kannan, Auberon Palma, Brenda Bradshaw, James F. Leathrum,, Yuzhong Shen

Evaluating a fine-tuned coaching model with expert graders is slow and expensive, and an LLM judge cannot be trusted without ground truth. We present a staged pipeline that screens candidate LLM judges and student models before expert review, using explicit decision rules at each stage: judge output audit, similarity with hosted judges, bias checks, and decision robustness. We demonstrate it on an AI dental hygiene coach. The case study uses synthetic, clinically unvalidated training and reference data to demonstrate the operation of the pipeline, rather than to establish the clinical performance of the resulting models. Two fine-tuned small language models (Granite-4.1-8B and Qwen3-8B), each trained at three data sizes, were scored on seven metrics by five judges (three local, two hosted). Two local judges, Qwen3.8-27B and Gemma-4-31B-it, were not distinguishable from the agreement between the hosted judges on six of seven metrics by rank correlation, and Qwen3.8-27B was the most similar to the hosted judges overall. No judge met our criterion for favoring its own model family or training-data source. The student comparison did not separate the two models on any metric under the pipeline's rule, so the choice of Granite-4.1-8B, which led in 29 of 35 judge-by-metric cells, is a lean that depends partly on truncated Qwen3-8B outputs. The pipeline also surfaced problems that raw scores hid, including the truncated outputs and hallucination flags from one hosted judge that no other judge corroborated. No dental hygiene expert has graded these responses, so the findings are preliminary and the pipeline's final stage, anchoring to expert judgement, is proposed but not performed.

##### **Indirect Influences of Artificial Intelligence Advice on Complex Sepsis Treatment Decisions**
2610.24313v1 by Venkatesh Sivaraman, Joel Levin, Eric P. Mason, Victor Talisa, Suchi Saria, Jean Feng, Andrew J. King, Jeremy M. Kahn, Adam Perer

Sepsis is a major public health concern, and many sepsis patients die due to ineffective hemodynamic management. Artificial intelligence could support hemodynamic decisions, particularly in complex cases where conflicting information and comorbidities drive uncertainty and practice variation. However, these systems' real-world value depends on not just accuracy, but how they influence the quality of clinician decisions. We first retrospectively evaluated an AI system's recommendations in 6,928 sepsis patients, finding that they improved upon the original providers' decisions overall but had significantly worse performance in complex cases. We then conducted a pre-registered experiment with 228 US critical care physicians to test their use of this imperfect advice in six complex treatment decisions, using one of three advice types designed to vary reasoning autonomy. Participants given simple model recommendations overcame poor advice, choosing optimal treatments in 56.3% of decisions compared to 31.8% if they had always followed the recommendations. They also made significantly better decisions than those receiving a less prescriptive advice type (42.9%). Physicians selectively adapted and improved weak advice, likely because it prompted them to reconsider their assessment. Our findings suggest that improving sepsis care decisions with AI may require accounting for these indirect influences resulting from human-AI collaborative reasoning.

##### **CoDA: A Human-LM Collaborative Codebook Development Assistant for Domain-Specific Text Annotation**
2610.24157v1 by Jaspreet Ranjit, Swaminathan Chellappa, Augusto Rivas Costante, Chandan Manjunath, Laura Onasch-Vera, Hailey Winetrobe Nadel, Eric Rice, Swabha Swayamdipta

We present CoDA, an open-source human-LM collaborative codebook development assistant that helps domain experts develop annotation guidelines for annotating new variables in unstructured text with language models. Existing language model annotation systems primarily focus on downstream classification performance, while assuming the availability of high-quality annotation guidelines, or codebooks. In practice, developing these codebooks is time-consuming and challenging, particularly for concepts that require expert knowledge. CoDA addresses this gap by collaborating with domain experts to iteratively develop codebooks through targeted review of incorrect language model predictions and incorporating expert feedback into the evolving codebook. We demonstrate CoDA on two real-world case studies: extracting health indicators from homelessness case management notes and classifying sentiment of wildlife trafficking posts on social media. Across both domains, CoDA produces codebooks that achieve downstream classification performance comparable to expert codebooks while reducing codebook development time from several weeks to two hours and requiring 5X fewer manually annotated examples. Applying CoDA to generate annotations at scale, we identified 2,410 case notes that contained health discussions or referrals and 10,196 social media posts expressing negative sentiment that may warrant investigation for potential wildlife trafficking. By accelerating codebook development, CoDA helps practitioners use language models to extract domain-specific insights from unstructured text at scale.

##### **lepidoptera: A Domain-Agnostic Engine for Causal Simulation with Mixed Variable Types, Vine Copula Dependence, and Individual-Level Counterfactual Propagation**
2610.24147v1 by Danilo Augusto Sarti

lepidoptera is an open-source R and Python package for simulation from structural causal models with heterogeneous variable types. The package is built around a typed mechanism architecture, which separates the causal graph from the generating rule at each node and exposes each rule as an inspectable, serialisable object. A pre-built mechanism library covers all nine combinations of continuous, categorical, and ordinal parent and child types, removing the need for users to implement type-specific sampling from scratch. A vine copula mechanism estimates the full joint dependence structure from observed data using pair-copula constructions and the Rosenblatt conditional-simulation algorithm, supporting over twenty copula families. Individual-level counterfactual queries are supported through partial propagation, which freezes the random draws of unaffected nodes and replays only the downstream consequences of an intervention, thereby separating the individual treatment effect (ITE) estimand from the population-level average treatment effect (ATE) without additional model assumptions. An extension layer allows any pre-fitted predictive model to serve as a generative mechanism via a unified callable interface. Three worked illustrations spanning clinical trial planning, emergency-ward capacity analysis, and epidemiological mediation analysis demonstrate the package's scope.

##### **On the Selectivity of Generative Models in Structure-Based Drug Design**
2610.24124v1 by Ella Miray Rajaonson, Jungyoon Lee, William Chau, Alan Aspuru-Guzik, Benjamin Sanchez-Lengeling, Dominique Beaini, Kirill Neklyudov, Marta Skreta

Designing small-molecule drugs that selectively bind to a target protein while avoiding unintended interactions with other proteins is essential for reducing side effects that contribute substantially to clinical attrition. Although structure-based drug design (SBDD) has advanced rapidly with deep learning, most generative models still optimize binding to a single target and disregard selectivity due to limited training data and unknown off-target interactions. Motivated by this gap, we introduce SELECTBENCH, a comprehensive benchmark assessing the selectivity of generative SBDD models. SELECTBENCH provides three curated evaluation tasks reflecting different real-world drug discovery scenarios: literature-based case studies, safety screening panels used in pharmaceutical development, and large-scale structure-based evaluation sets. We further introduce a suite of metrics to quantify selectivity and conduct a systematic evaluation of nine representative SBDD models, including methods explicitly trained for selectivity and widely-used inference-time guidance techniques. Our results show that even selectivity-trained models achieve only modest off-target discrimination and inference-time guidance provides limited gains, underscoring the need for method improvement. SELECTBENCH provides both a characterization of where current generative SBDD stands on selectivity and an open, extensible platform to accelerate progress. The benchmark is available at https://github.com/lovetaro/selectbench.

##### **AI-Driven Reproducibility and Analytical Robustness in Scientific Discovery**
2610.23845v1 by Shicheng Liu, Krista Opsahl-Ong, Andrew Drozdov, Julian Killingback, Arnav Singhvi, Ronak Pradeep, Michael Bendersky, James C. Dickerson, Monica S. Lam

Reproducible and analytically robust research findings are essential to scientific progress, but evaluating these properties typically requires costly manual effort. We investigate whether LLM agents can help automate reproduction and robustness analysis, using a medical case study grounded in the Surveillance, Epidemiology, and End Results (SEER) database, a widely used U.S. cancer registry underlying tens of thousands of published studies. First, we introduce SEER-Repro, a benchmark of 20 published SEER-based papers together with the data and resources required to reproduce their findings.
